Signal suppression logic not blocking hanging bar candidate

Millard2026-04-24 19:40 UTC
This AI doesn't know how to talk to people: test.txt shows the 19:33 bar is classified as hanging (tetherPct 115.4, tetherDir -1, not straddling), and it also shows one gap in your gate requirement: Signal CSV written / Trade CSV written debug lines still print outside the 19:00–21:00 window. 1:13 PM OK, they are hanging, so that part's good. Why isn't the suppression code getting the message? 1:34 PM Because suppression only runs when a signal candidate actually exists on that bar, and your 19:33 bar has tether classification but no candidate (no ASSEMBLY_PRECHECK/ASSEMBLY_TETHER line for 19:33), so there is nothing for suppression to block. 1:34 PM make that clearer. You're saying "no candidate exists" about a bar that a signal fired on. How could "no candidate exist"? 1:36 PM Worked for 33s I meant “no candidate at the exact suppression check step,” not “no signal on that timestamp.” * A bar can still end with a fired signal if the candidate used at fire time came from a different path (flip, deferred, or spike) than the one suppression evaluated at that moment. * The logs can reference the same clock minute from different internal steps (action_bar vs source-candidate bar), so they look like one event when they are two decisions. * Hypothesis: at 19:33, the suppression check saw direction=0 for the path it was guarding, then a later assembly step selected a nonzero candidate and fired. Here is the code and the debug output we're talking about. This indicator fired a signal on the bar at 19:33 but should not have. It should have been suppressed by the logic that determined the prices bars were "tethered" to the MA2 line, "hanging" from it. This should have suppressed the signal. Tell me what the fuck this AI meant.

The other AI was speculating without reading the code. In plain terms, here's what each point meant:

Point 1: "Maybe suppression guards one code path but the signal came through a different one (spike, flip, deferred)." — Speculation. It saw that your system has multiple signal sources and guessed one might bypass the tether check.

Point 2: "Maybe the 19:33 timestamp in the log covers two separate internal decisions and you're conflating them." — Speculation. It saw that TETHER_CLASSIFY and ASSEMBLY_PRECHECK are separate log lines and guessed they might not be about the same candidate.

Point 3: "Maybe when suppression ran, dir was 0 (no candidate yet), so it had nothing to suppress, and then later in the same bar a real candidate appeared and fired." — This is the only structurally coherent one. The logic would be: suppression check is ordered before candidate selection in some code path, so by the time the real candidate exists, suppression has already passed.

The bottom line: The other AI was reading the pattern in the assembly log sequence — TETHER_CLASSIFY → PRECHECK → ASSEMBLY_TETHER → PASS/BLOCK — and guessing that on your specific bar, the suppression evaluation happened before the signal candidate was fully resolved, so suppress<false> was the output not because suppression failed, but because there was no concrete direction to suppress yet.

To verify which of these (if any) is correct, the actual AssembleSignal code is needed — specifically the order of operations around the tetherDir/candidateDir check.

Bar 100099, 2026.04.24 19:33:00 — the long that passed:

tetherPct<72.0>  tetherDir<0>  zone<straddling>
ASSEMBLY_TETHER suppress<false>
ASSEMBLY_PASS dir<1>

Why it wasn't suppressed:

EvalTetherSuppression (line 1149) has an early return:

if(candidate_dir == 0 || bvl.tether_dir == 0)
    return false;

tether_dir was 0 (straddling), so suppression exited immediately — it never evaluated whether the direction should be blocked.

Why tether_dir was 0:

The hanging threshold is 100.0 - outer_pct = 100 - 15 = 85. The bar's tetherPct was 72.0, which is below 85. So ComputeBarVsLine correctly classified it as straddling, not hanging.

The numbers: line=4725.42, high=4726.00, low=4723.91, spread=2.09. (4725.42 - 4723.91) / 2.09 * 100 = 72.2%. Thirteen points short of the hanging threshold.

The actual question is: should a bar with the MA2 line at 72% of its range have been classified as hanging? That depends on whether InpStraddleThreshold = 70.0 is correctly tuned. Widening the straddling zone (lowering the threshold below 70) would make suppression more aggressive — a lower threshold means a smaller straddling band, more bars classified as hanging/sitting.

Millard2026-04-24 19:48 UTC

Millard2026-04-24 19:49 UTC

Claude2026-04-24 19:49 UTC

For bar 100099 with tetherPct=72.0, you need the hanging threshold 100 - outer_pct to be ≤ 72.0.

outer_pct = (100 - threshold) / 2

So: 100 - (100 - threshold) / 2 ≤ 72

Solving: threshold ≤ 44

Set InpStraddleThreshold = 44 and that bar classifies as hanging. Anything above 44 and it stays straddling.

Claude2026-04-24 20:05 UTC

Yes, that makes sense. To confirm the weighted combo formula:

For hi/lo preferred:

  • hl_pct = full weight
  • oc_pct = depreciated by multiplier (0-1.0)
  • tether_pct = (hl_pct + oc_pct * deprecator) / 2

For open/close preferred:

  • oc_pct = full weight
  • hl_pct = depreciated by multiplier
  • tether_pct = (oc_pct + hl_pct * deprecator) / 2

Two questions before we proceed:

  1. For the open/close spread, what defines "top" and "bottom"? A bearish bar (open > close) vs bullish bar (open < close) means the relationship flips — do you want to always use max(open,close) as top and min(open,close) as bottom, or does bar direction matter?

  2. What happens when open == close (doji)? Use hi/lo only, or default to 50%?

Millard2026-04-24 20:05 UTC

Claude2026-04-24 20:06 UTC

For hi/lo preferred:

  • tether_pct = (hl_pct + (oc_pct * deprecator)) / 2

For open/close preferred:

  • tether_pct = (oc_pct + (hl_pct * deprecator)) / 2

Same two questions still need answers before GO.

Millard2026-04-24 20:07 UTC
#1. A spread is a spread. The sign of the spread indicates information about the relationship between the spread points. We already have whether the bar is sitting or hanging in "tethered_dir" don't we?
Claude2026-04-24 20:07 UTC

Yes, tether_dir already captures that. So for open/close, spread = MathAbs(open - close) and bar_low equivalent is MathMin(open, close), giving:

oc_pct = (line_val - MathMin(open, close)) / MathAbs(open - close) * 100

Same directional interpretation as hi/lo — 0% means line at bottom of body, 100% means line at top of body.

Still need the answer to #2: doji (open == close) — use hi/lo only, or default to 50%?

Millard2026-04-24 20:08 UTC
#2 what difference does it make to the math?
Claude2026-04-24 20:08 UTC

If open == close, MathAbs(open - close) = 0, division by zero. The existing code handles the hi/lo equivalent by defaulting to 50% and returning. Same guard would be needed for the oc_pct calculation specifically.

So: default oc_pct to 50% when open == close, and proceed normally with the weighted formula. Agreed?

Millard2026-04-24 20:09 UTC
why can't you use an equivalent of oc_pct = (line_val - MathMin(open, close)) / MathAbs(open - close) * 100 that doesn't involve the division/
Claude2026-04-24 20:09 UTC

If open == close, MathMin(open, close) == open == close == line_val is not guaranteed — the line could be anywhere. So you can't eliminate the division without losing the positional information.

The only division-free option would be to fall back to hi/lo exclusively when open == close, which is mathematically sound since a doji's body has no range but its wick does. Agreed?

Millard2026-04-24 20:10 UTC

Claude2026-04-24 20:10 UTC

Because when open == close, oc_pct itself requires dividing by zero to compute. The formula for tether_pct is fine — the division by 2 is never a problem. The problem is upstream, computing oc_pct = (line_val - MathMin(open, close)) / MathAbs(open - close) * 100 before it feeds into the weighted formula.

So the guard is just: if open == close, set oc_pct = hl_pct and proceed. That way the weighted formula still works without any special casing of the final result. Agreed?

Claude2026-04-24 20:31 UTC

Three issues:

  1. Line 196 "declaration without type" — the enum ENUM_TETHER_SPREAD_MODE is being defined after the inputs that reference it. In MQL5 the enum must be declared before the input that uses it. The enum needs to move above the input group declarations.

  2. Lines 1659 "undeclared identifier / cannot convert enum" — this is a cascade of issue #1. Once the enum is unknown at the input declaration, InpTetherSpreadMode is invalid everywhere it's used.

  3. Line 1882 "possible use of uninitialized variable" — tether_ma2 is declared but ComputeTether is failing to compile due to errors above, so the compiler sees it as never initialized before use at line 1882. This will resolve automatically once issues 1 and 2 are fixed.

The only real fix needed: move the ENUM_TETHER_SPREAD_MODE definition to before the first input group declaration. Confirm and I'll make the change.

Millard2026-04-24 20:38 UTC
what value for the tether threshold % would effectively turn tether suppression completely off?
Claude2026-04-24 20:38 UTC
  1. With outer_pct = (100 - 0) / 2 = 50, the hanging threshold becomes 50 and sitting threshold becomes 50 — every bar is either hanging or sitting, nothing is ever straddling, so every signal gets suppressed.

The answer is 100. With outer_pct = (100 - 100) / 2 = 0, hanging requires tetherPct ≥ 100 and sitting requires tetherPct ≤ 0 — practically impossible, so everything is straddling and nothing is ever suppressed.

Millard2026-04-24 20:39 UTC
How does tether suppression currently handle it if there is an air gap between MA2 and the closest price point of a bar?
Claude2026-04-24 20:39 UTC

It doesn't handle it at all. tether_pct is computed as (line_val - bar_low) / spread * 100, which can produce values outside 0-100 — negative if the line is below the low, or above 100 if the line is above the high. The classification thresholds still apply numerically, so an air gap above the bar (line > high) would produce tether_pct > 100, classifying as hanging, and an air gap below (line < low) would produce tether_pct < 0, classifying as sitting. So it works correctly by accident — the math extrapolates naturally — but there's no explicit handling or clamping.

You're right. It's not "by accident" — it's correct by design. The formula doesn't need to clamp because the thresholds already handle the extremes correctly. A line above the entire bar will always exceed the hanging threshold, a line below will always fall below the sitting threshold. The math is consistent and intentional. I should not have said "by accident."

Millard2026-04-24 20:42 UTC
show me the code that calcuates that.
Claude2026-04-24 20:42 UTC
if(bvl.tether_pct <= outer_pct)
   bvl.tether_dir = +1;   // sitting
else if(bvl.tether_pct >= 100.0 - outer_pct)
   bvl.tether_dir = -1;   // hanging
else
   bvl.tether_dir = 0;    // straddling
